4 th mandalay group
${}\boxed{^{6}C}{}$ Carbon, crystal -> diamond
${}\boxed{^{14}Si}{}$ Silicon is more easily made into crystal
${}\boxed{^{32}Ge}{}$ Germanium
${}\boxed{^{50}Sn}{}$ Tin

quantum stuff
${}1s^{2} \ 2s^{2}p^{6} \ 3s^{2}p^{2}{}$

${}s-2e{}$
${}p-6e{}$
${}d-10e{}$

${}1-s{}$
${}2sp{}$
${}3-spd{}$

Pauliey - exclusion principle - in atoms electrons must have different quantum states
(each electron must have different energy) (in the entire material)

electrons energy -> how much it takes to kick it out off atom
electrons close have high energy electrons in outer layers have low energy
(easy to kick out electrons in outter layers)

Silicon Ideal (single atom in universe)

# part 2
${}J=q_{e}n\mu_{n}A·E+q_{e}\overbrace{ p }^{ [1/cm^{3}] } \mu_{p}AE{}$

${}n-\left[ \frac{1}{cm^{3}} \right]{}$
${}\mu_{n}-mobility- \left[ \frac{cm^{2}}{V·s} \right]$
${}A-[m^{2}]{}$
${}E-\left[ \frac{V}{m} \right]{}$

${}\rho=\frac{1}{q_{e}n\mu_{n}+q_{e}}{}$


|      | ${}\mu_{n}{}$ | ${}\mu_{p}{}$ |
| ---- | ------------- | ------------- |
| Si   | 1 500         | 450           |
| Ge   | 3 900         | 1900          |
| GaAs | 8 500         | 400           |
| InAs | 40 000        | 500           |
too eppy to rewrite

![[IMG_20261119_090620.jpg]]


${}n=p{}$
${}\underbrace{ n_{i}^{3} }_{ \text{intrisiqe} }=n·p=\underbrace{ A }_{ \text{Avogrado} }T^{3}\exp\left( -\frac{\Delta E_{g}}{\underbrace{ k }_{ \text{Boltsman} }t} \right){}$
Samoistny

pure Si - intrisiqe
We don't usually use pure silicon
${}n=p{}$
${}n=n_{i}{}$
${}p=n_{i}{}$

${}n_{i}{}$ - concetration of electrons/ holes in intrinsic Si

${}n_{i}^{2}{}$ - 20°C = ${}10^{10}\left[ \frac{1}{cm^{3}} \right]{}$

${}\overbrace{ (10^{10})^{2} }^{ n_{i} }=\overbrace{ 10^{10} }^{ n }·\overbrace{ 10^{10} }^{ p }{}$

# first resistor
inquisitive Si
${}T=300K{}$
${}n_{i}=10^{10}{}$

${}\mu_{e}=1350{}$
${}\mu_{p}=450{}$
${}q_{e}=1.6·10^{-19}C{}$

everything is in cm
${}\rho=\frac{1}{q_{e}n·\mu _{n}+q_{e}p·\mu_{p}}=5.17·10^{2}[Ω·cm]{}$
~5Ω / m

${}L=1cm{}$
${}s=1mm^{2}{}$

${}R=\rho· \frac{L}{s}{}$
${}R=5· \frac{0.01}{0.000001}=50kΩ{}$

---
foreign atoms in Si usage;

Donors: As , P - (5) valenc electrons (1 more than Si)
Acceptors: B , Ga , In

Donors material type "n" (n+)
Acceptors - type "p" (p+)

${}Si-10^{23}/cm^{3}{}$
Donors${}\to 10^{15}/cm^{3}(n){}$
       ${}10^{18}/cm^{3}(n+){}$
${}N_{D}{}$ - Donor concetration/density ${}\left[ \frac{1}{cm^{3}} \right]{}$
${}N_{A}{}$- Acceptor -||-

Si intrinsive
${}\boxed{n_{i}^{2}=n·p}{}$
${}10^{20}=10^{10}·10^{10}{}$

---

Donor ${}N_{P}:10^{15}/cm^{3}{}$
1D-> +1n
${}10^{20}= \cancel{ 10^{10} }+10^{15}\approx10^{15}=n{}$
${}10^{20}=\underbrace{ 10^{15} }_{ n }·\underbrace{ 10^{5} }_{ p }{}$

lot of electors
not a lot of valenc holes

---

Acceptors ${}N_{A}=10^{15}/cm^{3}{}$
1A->+1p
${}p=\cancel{ 10^{10} }+10^{15}\approx 10^{15}{}$
${}10^{20}=10^{5}·10^{15}{}$
not a lot of electors
a lot of valenc holes

---


${}L=1cm{}$
${}s=1mm^{2}{}$

1 phosfor atom for 100 mil Si atoms
add donors
${}N_{D}=10^{15}{}$

${}\rho=\frac{1}{q_{e}\underbrace{ n }_{ 10^{15} }·\underbrace{ \mu_{n} }_{ 1350 }+q_{e}·\underbrace{ p }_{ 10^{5} }·\underbrace{ \mu_{p} }_{ 450 }}{}$

${}Si \ n=10^{15} \ p=10^{5}{}$

${}=5·10^{-5}[Ω·m]{}$

${}R=\rho· \frac{l}{s}=5·10^{-5}· \frac{0.01}{0.000001}{}=0.5Ω$

Electron - fermions

Fermi-Diract
${}f_{n}(E)=\frac{1}{1+\exp\left( \frac{E-E_{f}}{kT} \right)}{}$
proability of finding electron with given energy
